The Islamist militants conducted a violent campaign of [civilian massacres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_massacres_during_the_Algerian_Civil_War "List of massacres during the Algerian Civil War").[\[158\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-165)\[*[failed verification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability "Wikipedia:Verifiability")*] At several points in the conflict, the situation in Algeria became a point of international concern, most notably during the crisis surrounding [Air France Flight 8969](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air_France_Flight_8969 "Air France Flight 8969"), a hijacking perpetrated by the Armed Islamic Group. The Armed Islamic Group declared a ceasefire in October 1997.[\[156\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-faco-163)

Algeria held [elections in 1999](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algerian_presidential_election,_1999 "Algerian presidential election, 1999"), considered biased by international observers and most opposition groups[\[159\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-166) which were won by President [Abdelaziz Bouteflika](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdelaziz_Bouteflika "Abdelaziz Bouteflika"). He worked to restore political stability to the country and announced a "Civil Concord" initiative, approved in a [referendum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algerian_Civil_Concord_referendum,_1999 "Algerian Civil Concord referendum, 1999"), under which many political prisoners were pardoned, and several thousand members of armed groups were granted exemption from prosecution under a limited amnesty, in force until 13 January 2000. The AIS disbanded and levels of insurgent violence fell rapidly. The [Groupe Salafiste pour la Prédication et le Combat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Groupe_Salafiste_pour_la_Pr%C3%A9dication_et_le_Combat "Groupe Salafiste pour la Prédication et le Combat") (GSPC), a splinter group of the Armed Islamic Group, continued a terrorist campaign against the Government.[\[156\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-faco-163)

Bouteflika was re-elected in the [April 2004 presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algerian_presidential_election,_2004 "Algerian presidential election, 2004") after campaigning on a programme of national reconciliation. The programme comprised economic, institutional, political and social reform to modernise the country, raise living standards, and tackle the causes of alienation. It also included a second amnesty initiative, the [Charter for Peace and National Reconciliation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charter_for_Peace_and_National_Reconciliation "Charter for Peace and National Reconciliation"), which was approved in a [referendum in September 2005](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algerian_national_reconciliation_referendum,_2005 "Algerian national reconciliation referendum, 2005"). It offered amnesty to most guerrillas and Government security forces.[\[156\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-faco-163)

In November 2008, the [Algerian Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algerian_Constitution "Algerian Constitution") was amended following a vote in Parliament, removing the two-term limit on Presidential incumbents. This change enabled Bouteflika to stand for re-election in the [2009 presidential elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algerian_presidential_election,_2009 "Algerian presidential election, 2009"), and he was re-elected in April 2009. During his election campaign and following his re-election, Bouteflika promised to extend the programme of national reconciliation and a $150-billion spending programme to create three million new jobs, the construction of one million new housing units, and to continue public sector and infrastructure modernisation programmes.[\[156\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-faco-163)

A continuing series of protests throughout the country started on 28 December 2010, inspired by similar [protests across the Middle East and North Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Spring "Arab Spring"). On 24 February 2011, the government lifted Algeria's 19-year-old [state of emergency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_of_emergency "State of emergency").[\[160\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-167) The government enacted legislation dealing with political parties, the electoral code, and the representation of women in elected bodies.[\[161\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-aaeo-168) In April 2011, Bouteflika promised further constitutional and political reform.[\[156\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-faco-163) However, elections are routinely criticised by opposition groups as unfair and international human rights groups say that media censorship and harassment of political opponents continue.

On 2 April 2019, Bouteflika resigned from the presidency after [mass protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019%E2%80%932020_Algerian_protests "2019–2020 Algerian protests") against his candidacy for a fifth term in office.[\[162\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-169)

In December 2019, [Abdelmadjid Tebboune](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdelmadjid_Tebboune "Abdelmadjid Tebboune") became Algeria's president, after winning the first round of the [presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Algerian_presidential_election "2019 Algerian presidential election") with a record abstention rate – the highest of all presidential elections since Algeria's democracy in 1989. Tebboune is accused of being close to the military and being loyal to the deposed president. Tebboune rejects these accusations, claiming to be the victim of a witch hunt. He also reminds his detractors that he was expelled from the Government in August 2017 at the instigation of oligarchs languishing in prison.[\[163\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-170) In September 2024, President Tebboune [won](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Algerian_presidential_election "2024 Algerian presidential election") a second term with a landslide 84.3 percent of the vote, although his opponents called the results fraud.[\[164\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-171)

## Geography

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Algeria\&action=edit\&section=10 "Edit section: Geography")]

Main article: [Geography of Algeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography_of_Algeria "Geography of Algeria")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c5/Algeria_relief.png/220px-Algeria_relief.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Algeria_relief.png)

The [Sahara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sahara "Sahara"), the [Hoggar Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hoggar_Mountains "Hoggar Mountains") and the [Atlas Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlas_Mountains "Atlas Mountains") compose the Algerian relief.

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a4/Dunes_of_Algeria.jpg/220px-Dunes_of_Algeria.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Dunes_of_Algeria.jpg)

The [Algerian Desert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algerian_Desert "Algerian Desert") makes up more than 90% of the country's total area.[\[165\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-172)

Since the 2011 breakup of Sudan, and the creation of South Sudan, Algeria has been the largest country in Africa, and the [Mediterranean Basin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mediterranean_Basin "Mediterranean Basin"). Its southern part includes a significant portion of the [Sahara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sahara "Sahara"). To the north, the [Tell Atlas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tell_Atlas "Tell Atlas") forms with the [Saharan Atlas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saharan_Atlas "Saharan Atlas"), further south, two parallel sets of reliefs in approaching eastbound, and between which are inserted vast plains and highlands. Both Atlas tend to merge in eastern Algeria. The vast mountain ranges of [Aures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aures "Aures") and [Nememcha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T%C3%A9bessa "Tébessa") occupy the entire northeastern Algeria and are delineated by the Tunisian border. The highest point is [Mount Tahat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Tahat "Mount Tahat") (3,003 metres or 9,852 feet).

Algeria lies mostly between latitudes [19°](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/19th_parallel_north "19th parallel north") and [37°N](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/37th_parallel_north "37th parallel north") (a small area is north of 37°N and south of 19°N), and longitudes [9°W](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/9th_meridian_west "9th meridian west") and [12°E](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/12th_meridian_east "12th meridian east"). Most of the coastal area is hilly, sometimes even mountainous, and there are a few natural [harbours](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harbour "Harbour"). The area from the coast to the Tell Atlas is fertile. South of the Tell Atlas is a [steppe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steppe "Steppe") landscape ending with the [Saharan Atlas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saharan_Atlas "Saharan Atlas"); farther south, there is the Sahara desert.[\[166\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-LOC-173)

The [Hoggar Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hoggar_Mountains "Hoggar Mountains") ([Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_language "Arabic language"): جبال هقار), also known as the Hoggar, are a highland region in central Sahara, southern Algeria. They are located about 1,500 km (932 mi) south of the capital, Algiers, and just east of [Tamanghasset](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamanghasset "Tamanghasset"). Algiers, [Oran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oran "Oran"), [Constantine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constantine,_Algeria "Constantine, Algeria"), and [Annaba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annaba "Annaba") are Algeria's main cities.[\[166\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-LOC-173)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7c/Djurdjura_mountains.jpg/220px-Djurdjura_mountains.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Djurdjura_mountains.jpg)

The Babor mountains and the Jijel Coast. The Erraguene lake can be seen on the right.

### Climate and hydrology

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Algeria\&action=edit\&section=11 "Edit section: Climate and hydrology")]

Main article: [Climate of Algeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_of_Algeria "Climate of Algeria")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/92/Koppen-Geiger_Map_DZA_present.svg/220px-Koppen-Geiger_Map_DZA_present.svg.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Koppen-Geiger_Map_DZA_present.svg)

Algeria map of [Köppen climate classification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K%C3%B6ppen_climate_classification "Köppen climate classification").

In this region, midday desert temperatures can be hot year round. After sunset, however, the clear, dry air permits rapid loss of heat, and the nights are cool to chilly. Enormous daily ranges in temperature are recorded.

Rainfall is fairly plentiful along the coastal part of the Tell Atlas, ranging from 400 to 670 mm (15.7 to 26.4 in) annually, the amount of precipitation increasing from west to east. [Precipitation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Precipitation_\(meteorology\) "Precipitation (meteorology)") is heaviest in the northern part of eastern Algeria, where it reaches as much as 1,000 mm (39.4 in) in some years.

Farther inland, the rainfall is less plentiful. Algeria also has [ergs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erg_\(landform\) "Erg (landform)"), or sand dunes, between mountains. Among these, in the summer time when winds are heavy and gusty, temperatures can go up to 43.3 °C (110 °F).

[Climate change in Algeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_change_in_Algeria "Climate change in Algeria") has wide-reaching effects on the country. Algeria was not a significant contributor to climate change,[\[167\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Climate_change_in_Algeria_:0al-174) but, like other countries in the [Middle East and North Africa (MENA) region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_East_and_North_Africa "Middle East and North Africa"), is expected to be among the most affected by [climate change impacts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Effects_of_climate_change "Effects of climate change").[\[168\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-175) Because a large part of the country is in already hot and [arid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aridity "Aridity") geographies, including part of the [Sahara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sahara "Sahara"), already strong heat and [water resource](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_resource "Water resource") access challenges are expected to get worse.[\[167\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Climate_change_in_Algeria_:0al-174) As early as 2014, scientists were attributing extreme [heat waves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heat_wave "Heat wave") to climate change in Algeria.[\[167\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Climate_change_in_Algeria_:0al-174) Algeria was ranked 46th of countries in the 2020 [Climate Change Performance Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_Change_Performance_Index "Climate Change Performance Index").[\[169\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-176)

### Fauna and flora

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Algeria\&action=edit\&section=12 "Edit section: Fauna and flora")]

Main article: [Wildlife of Algeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wildlife_of_Algeria "Wildlife of Algeria")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/54/Vulpes_zerda.JPG/220px-Vulpes_zerda.JPG)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Vulpes_zerda.JPG)

The [fennec fox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fennec_fox "Fennec fox") is the [national animal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_animal "National animal") of Algeria

The varied vegetation of Algeria includes [coastal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coastal "Coastal"), [mountainous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mountainous "Mountainous") and grassy [desert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Desert "Desert")-like regions which all support a wide range of wildlife.

In Algeria [forest cover](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forest_cover "Forest cover") is around 1% of the total land area, equivalent to 1,949,000 hectares (ha) of forest in 2020, up from 1,667,000 hectares (ha) in 1990. In 2020, naturally regenerating forest covered 1,439,000 hectares (ha) and planted forest covered 510,000 hectares (ha). Of the naturally regenerating forest 0% was reported to be [primary forest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Primary_forest "Primary forest") (consisting of native tree species with no clearly visible indications of human activity) and around 6% of the forest area was found within protected areas. For the year 2015, 80% of the forest area was reported to be under [public ownership](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_ownership "State ownership"), 18% [private ownership](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Private_property "Private property") and 2% with ownership listed as other or unknown.[\[170\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-177)[\[171\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-178)

Many of the creatures constituting the Algerian wildlife live in close proximity to civilisation. The most commonly seen animals include the wild [boars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boar "Boar"), [jackals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jackal "Jackal"), and [gazelles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gazelle "Gazelle"), although it is not uncommon to spot [fennecs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fennecs "Fennecs") (foxes), and [jerboas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jerboas "Jerboas"). Algeria also has a small [African leopard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African_leopard "African leopard") and [Saharan cheetah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saharan_cheetah "Saharan cheetah") population, but these are seldom seen. A species of deer, the [Barbary stag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barbary_stag "Barbary stag"), inhabits the dense humid forests in the north-eastern areas. The [fennec fox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fennec_fox "Fennec fox") is the [national animal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_animal "National animal") of Algeria.[\[172\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-179)

A variety of bird species makes the country an attraction for bird watchers. The forests are inhabited by boars and jackals. [Barbary macaques](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barbary_macaque "Barbary macaque") are the sole native monkey. Snakes, [monitor lizards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monitor_lizard "Monitor lizard"), and numerous other reptiles can be found living among an array of [rodents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rodent "Rodent") throughout the [semi arid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Semi_arid "Semi arid") regions of Algeria. Many animals are now extinct, including the [Barbary lions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barbary_lion "Barbary lion"), [Atlas bears](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlas_bear "Atlas bear") and [crocodiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_African_crocodile "West African crocodile").[\[173\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-180)

In the north, some of the native flora includes [Macchia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macchia "Macchia") scrub, [olive trees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olive "Olive"), [oaks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oak "Oak"), [cedars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cedrus "Cedrus") and other [conifers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pinophyta "Pinophyta"). The mountain regions contain large forests of evergreens ([Aleppo pine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aleppo_pine "Aleppo pine"), [juniper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juniper "Juniper"), and [evergreen oak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evergreen_oak "Evergreen oak")) and some deciduous trees. [Fig](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ficus "Ficus"), [eucalyptus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eucalyptus "Eucalyptus"), [agave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agave "Agave"), and various [palm trees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arecaceae "Arecaceae") grow in the warmer areas. The [grape vine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grape_vine "Grape vine") is indigenous to the coast. In the Sahara region, some oases have palm trees. [Acacias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acacia "Acacia") with wild [olives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olive "Olive") are the predominant flora in the remainder of the [Sahara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sahara "Sahara"). Algeria had a 2018 [Forest Landscape Integrity Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forest_Landscape_Integrity_Index "Forest Landscape Integrity Index") mean score of 5.22/10, ranking it 106th globally out of 172 countries.[\[174\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FLII-Supplementary-181)

Camels are used extensively; the desert also abounds with venomous and nonvenomous snakes, [scorpions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scorpion "Scorpion"), and numerous insects.

## Government and politics

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Algeria\&action=edit\&section=13 "Edit section: Government and politics")]

Main article: [Politics of Algeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_Algeria "Politics of Algeria")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fb/Abdelmadjid_Tebboune_%282023%29_%28cropped%29.jpg/170px-Abdelmadjid_Tebboune_%282023%29_%28cropped%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Abdelmadjid_Tebboune_\(2023\)_\(cropped\).jpg)

[Abdelmadjid Tebboune](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdelmadjid_Tebboune "Abdelmadjid Tebboune"), President of Algeria since 2019

Algeria's government has been described as authoritarian,[\[175\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-182)[\[176\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-183)[\[177\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-184)[\[178\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-185) and elected politicians have relatively little sway over affairs in the country. Instead, a group of unelected civilian and military "décideurs" ("deciders"), known as "le pouvoir" ("the power"), de facto rule the country, even deciding who should be president.[\[179\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-186)[\[180\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-187)[\[181\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-188) The most powerful man might have been [Mohamed Mediène](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohamed_Medi%C3%A8ne "Mohamed Mediène"), the head of military intelligence, before he was brought down during the [2019 protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019%E2%80%932020_Algerian_protests "2019–2020 Algerian protests").[\[182\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-189) In recent years, many of these generals have died, retired, or been imprisoned. After the death of General [Larbi Belkheir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Larbi_Belkheir "Larbi Belkheir"), previous president [Bouteflika](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdelaziz_Bouteflika "Abdelaziz Bouteflika") put loyalists in key posts, notably at [Sonatrach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sonatrach "Sonatrach"), and secured constitutional amendments that made him re-electable indefinitely, until he was brought down in 2019 during [protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019%E2%80%932020_Algerian_protests "2019–2020 Algerian protests").[\[183\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-190)

The head of state is the [President of Algeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_Algeria "President of Algeria"), who is elected for a five-year term. The president is limited to two five-year terms. The [most recent presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Algerian_presidential_election "2019 Algerian presidential election") was planned to be in April 2019, but [widespread protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Algerian_protests "2019 Algerian protests") erupted on 22 February against the president's decision to participate in the election, which resulted in President Bouteflika announcing his resignation on 3 April.[\[184\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-191) [Abdelmadjid Tebboune](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdelmadjid_Tebboune "Abdelmadjid Tebboune"), an independent candidate, was elected as president after the election eventually took place on 12 December 2019. Protestors refused to recognise Tebboune as president, citing demands for comprehensive reform of the political system.[\[185\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-192) Algeria has universal [suffrage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suffrage "Suffrage") at 18 years of age.[\[5\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-CIA-9) The President is the head of the [army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algerian_Army "Algerian Army"), the [Council of Ministers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_of_Ministers_of_Algeria "Council of Ministers of Algeria") and the [High Security Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=High_Security_Council\&action=edit\&redlink=1 "High Security Council (page does not exist)"). He appoints the [Prime Minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Algeria "Prime Minister of Algeria") who is also the head of government.[\[186\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-president-193)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/11/H%C3%A9micycle_de_l%27assembl%C3%A9e_populaire_nationale_%28Alg%C3%A9rie%29.jpg/220px-H%C3%A9micycle_de_l%27assembl%C3%A9e_populaire_nationale_%28Alg%C3%A9rie%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:H%C3%A9micycle_de_l%27assembl%C3%A9e_populaire_nationale_\(Alg%C3%A9rie\).jpg)

The [People's National Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People%27s_National_Assembly "People's National Assembly")

The Algerian parliament is [bicameral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bicameral "Bicameral"); the lower house, the [People's National Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People%27s_National_Assembly "People's National Assembly"), has 462 members who are directly elected for five-year terms, while the upper house, the [Council of the Nation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_of_the_Nation "Council of the Nation"), has 144 members serving six-year terms, of which 96 members are chosen by local assemblies and 48 are appointed by the president.[\[187\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-fitw13-194) According to the [constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Algeria "Constitution of Algeria"), no political association may be formed if it is "based on differences in religion, language, race, gender, profession, or region". In addition, political campaigns must be exempt from the aforementioned subjects.[\[188\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-195)

Parliamentary elections were last held in [May 2017](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Algerian_legislative_election "2017 Algerian legislative election"). In the elections, the [FLN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Liberation_Front_\(Algeria\) "National Liberation Front (Algeria)") lost 44 of its seats, but remained the largest party with 164 seats, the military-backed [National Rally for Democracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Rally_for_Democracy_\(Algeria\) "National Rally for Democracy (Algeria)") won 100, and the Muslim Brotherhood-linked [Movement of the Society for Peace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Movement_of_Society_for_Peace "Movement of Society for Peace") won 33.[\[189\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-196)

### Foreign relations

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Algeria\&action=edit\&section=14 "Edit section: Foreign relations")]

Main article: [Foreign relations of Algeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Algeria "Foreign relations of Algeria")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/58/Abdelaziz_Bouteflika_and_George_W_Bush_20080707.jpg/220px-Abdelaziz_Bouteflika_and_George_W_Bush_20080707.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Abdelaziz_Bouteflika_and_George_W_Bush_20080707.jpg)

President [Abdelaziz Bouteflika](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdelaziz_Bouteflika "Abdelaziz Bouteflika") and [George W. Bush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_W._Bush "George W. Bush") exchange handshakes at the Windsor Hotel Toya Resort and Spa in Tōyako Town, Abuta District, Hokkaidō in 2008. With them are [Dmitriy Medvedev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dmitriy_Medvedev "Dmitriy Medvedev"), left, and [Yasuo Fukuda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yasuo_Fukuda "Yasuo Fukuda"), right.

Algeria is included in the [European Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Union "European Union")'s [European Neighbourhood Policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Neighbourhood_Policy "European Neighbourhood Policy") (ENP) which aims at bringing the EU and its neighbours closer. Giving incentives and rewarding best performers, as well as offering funds in a faster and more flexible manner, are the two main principles underlying the European Neighbourhood Instrument (ENI) that came into force in 2014. It has a budget of €15.4 billion and provides the bulk of funding through a number of programmes.

In 2009, the French government agreed to compensate victims of nuclear tests in Algeria. Defence Minister Hervé Morin stated that "It's time for our country to be at peace with itself, at peace thanks to a system of compensation and reparations", when presenting the draft law on the payouts. Algerian officials and activists believe that this is a good first step and hope that this move would encourage broader reparation.[\[190\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-197)

[Tensions between Algeria and Morocco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algeria%E2%80%93Morocco_relations "Algeria–Morocco relations") in relation to the [Western Sahara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Sahara "Western Sahara") have been an obstacle to tightening the [Arab Maghreb Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Maghreb_Union "Arab Maghreb Union"), nominally established in 1989, but which has carried little practical weight.[\[191\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-198) On 24 August 2021, Algeria announced the break of diplomatic relations with Morocco.[\[192\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-199)

### Military

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Algeria\&action=edit\&section=15 "Edit section: Military")]

Main article: [Military of Algeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_of_Algeria "Military of Algeria")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3d/Al-chihab.jpg/220px-Al-chihab.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Al-chihab.jpg)

A [*Djebel Chenoua*-class corvette](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Djebel_Chenoua-class_corvette "Djebel Chenoua-class corvette"), designed and assembled in Algeria

The military of Algeria consists of the [People's National Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People%27s_National_Army "People's National Army") (ANP), the [Algerian National Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algerian_National_Navy "Algerian National Navy") (MRA), and the [Algerian Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algerian_Air_Force "Algerian Air Force") (QJJ), plus the [Territorial Air Defence Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Territorial_Air_Defence_Forces "Territorial Air Defence Forces").[\[15\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-AlgeriaFactbook-21) It is the direct successor of the [National Liberation Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Liberation_Army_\(Algeria\) "National Liberation Army (Algeria)") (Armée de Libération Nationale or ALN), the armed wing of the nationalist National Liberation Front which fought French colonial [occupation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_occupation "Military occupation") during the Algerian War of Independence (1954–62).

Total military personnel include 147,000 active, 150,000 reserve, and 187,000 paramilitary staff (2008 estimate).[\[193\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-IISS_TMB-200) Service in the military is compulsory for men aged 19–30, for a total of 12 months.[\[194\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-201) The military expenditure was 4.3% of the gross domestic product (GDP) in 2012.[\[15\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-AlgeriaFactbook-21) Algeria has the [second-largest military](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_number_of_military_and_paramilitary_personnel "List of countries by number of military and paramilitary personnel") in North Africa with the largest defence budget in Africa ($10 billion).[\[195\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-AlgeriaSpending-202) Most of Algeria's weapons are imported from Russia, with whom they [are a close ally](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algeria%E2%80%93Russia_relations "Algeria–Russia relations").[\[195\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-AlgeriaSpending-202)[\[196\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-203)

In 2007, the Algerian Air Force signed a deal with Russia to purchase 49 [MiG-29](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MiG-29 "MiG-29")SMT and 6 MiG-29UBT at an estimated cost of $1.9 billion. Russia is also building two [636-type](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kilo_class_submarine "Kilo class submarine") diesel [submarines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Submarines "Submarines") for Algeria.[\[197\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-204)

Algeria is the 90th most peaceful country in the world, according to the 2024 [Global Peace Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Peace_Index "Global Peace Index").[\[198\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-205)

### Human rights

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Algeria\&action=edit\&section=16 "Edit section: Human rights")]

Main article: [Human rights in Algeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_rights_in_Algeria "Human rights in Algeria")

Algeria has been categorised by the US government funded [Freedom House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_House "Freedom House") as "not free" since it began publishing such ratings in 1972, with the exception of 1989, 1990, and 1991, when the country was labelled "partly free".[\[199\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-206) In December 2016, the *Euro-Mediterranean Human Rights Monitor* issued a report regarding violation of [media freedom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_freedom "Media freedom") in Algeria. It clarified that the Algerian government imposed restrictions on [freedom of the press](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_of_the_press "Freedom of the press"); expression; and right to peaceful demonstration, protest and assembly as well as intensified censorship of the media and websites. Due to the fact that the journalists and activists criticise the ruling government, some media organisations' licenses are cancelled.[\[200\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-207)

Independent and autonomous trade unions face routine harassment from the government, with many leaders imprisoned and protests suppressed. In 2016, a number of unions, many of which were involved in the 2010–2012 Algerian Protests, have been deregistered by the government.[\[201\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-208)[\[202\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-209)[\[203\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-210)

[Homosexuality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_Algeria "LGBT rights in Algeria") is illegal in Algeria.[\[204\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-211) Public homosexual behavior is punishable by up to two years in prison.[\[205\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-212) Despite this, about 26% of Algerians think that homosexuality should be accepted, according to the survey conducted by the [BBC News Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BBC_Arabic "BBC Arabic")-Arab Barometer in 2019. Algeria showed the highest LGBT acceptance compared to other Arab countries where the survey was conducted.[\[206\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-213)

[Human Rights Watch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_Rights_Watch "Human Rights Watch") has accused the Algerian authorities of using the [COVID-19 pandemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic "COVID-19 pandemic") as an excuse to prevent pro-democracy movements and protests in the country, leading to the arrest of youths as part of [social distancing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_distancing "Social distancing").[\[207\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-214)

### Administrative divisions

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Algeria\&action=edit\&section=17 "Edit section: Administrative divisions")]

Main articles: [Provinces of Algeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Provinces_of_Algeria "Provinces of Algeria"), [Districts of Algeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Districts_of_Algeria "Districts of Algeria"), and [Municipalities of Algeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipalities_of_Algeria "Municipalities of Algeria")

Algeria is divided into 58 [provinces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Provinces_of_Algeria "Provinces of Algeria") (*[wilayas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilaya "Wilaya")*), 553 [districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Districts_of_Algeria "Districts of Algeria") (*[daïras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Da%C3%AFra "Daïra")*)[\[208\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-215) and 1,541 [municipalities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipalities_of_Algeria "Municipalities of Algeria") (*[baladiyahs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baladiyah "Baladiyah")*). Each province, district, and municipality is named after its [seat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capital_city "Capital city"), which is usually the largest city.

The administrative divisions have changed several times since independence. When introducing new provinces, the numbers of old provinces are kept, hence the non-alphabetical order. With their official numbers, currently (since 1983) they are:[\[15\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-AlgeriaFactbook-21)

## Economy

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Algeria\&action=edit\&section=18 "Edit section: Economy")]

Main article: [Economy of Algeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Algeria "Economy of Algeria")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fa/GDP_per_capita_development_in_Algeria.png/300px-GDP_per_capita_development_in_Algeria.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:GDP_per_capita_development_in_Algeria.png)

GDP per capita development in Algeria

Algeria's currency is the [dinar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algerian_dinar "Algerian dinar") (DZD). The economy remains dominated by the state, a legacy of the country's socialist post-independence development model. In June 2024 The World Bank's 2024 report marks a turning point for Algeria, which joins the select club of upper-middle-income countries. This economic rise, the result of an ambitious development strategy, places the country in the same category as emerging powers such as China, Brazil and Turkey[\[209\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-216)[\[210\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-217)[\[211\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-218) In recent years, the Algerian government has halted the privatisation of state-owned industries and imposed restrictions on imports and foreign involvement in its economy.[\[212\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-219) These restrictions are just starting to be lifted off recently although questions about Algeria's slowly-diversifying economy remain.[\[213\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-220)[\[214\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-221)

Algeria has struggled to develop industries outside hydrocarbons in part because of high costs and an inert state bureaucracy. The government's efforts to diversify the economy by attracting foreign and domestic investment outside the energy sector have done little to reduce high youth unemployment rates or to address housing shortages.[\[15\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-AlgeriaFactbook-21) The country is facing a number of short-term and medium-term problems, including the need to diversify the economy, strengthen political, economic and financial reforms, improve the business climate and reduce inequalities among regions.[\[161\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-aaeo-168)

A wave of economic protests in February and March 2011 prompted the Algerian government to offer more than $23 billion in public grants and retroactive salary and benefit increases. Public spending has increased by 27% annually during the past five years. The 2010–14 public-investment programme will cost US$286 billion, 40% of which will go to human development.[\[161\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-aaeo-168)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4a/A_Market_in_Algeria.jpg/220px-A_Market_in_Algeria.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:A_Market_in_Algeria.jpg)

[Street market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Street_market "Street market") in Algeria

Thanks to strong hydrocarbon revenues, Algeria has a cushion of $173 billion in [foreign currency reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_currency_reserves "Foreign currency reserves") and a large hydrocarbon stabilisation fund. In addition, Algeria's [external debt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_external_debt "List of countries by external debt") is extremely low at about 2% of GDP.[\[15\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-AlgeriaFactbook-21) The economy remains very dependent on hydrocarbon wealth, and, despite high foreign exchange reserves (US$178 billion, equivalent to three years of imports), current expenditure growth makes Algeria's budget more vulnerable to the risk of prolonged lower hydrocarbon revenues.[\[215\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-imfart4-222)

Algeria has not joined the [WTO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WTO "WTO"), despite several years of negotiations but is a member of the [Greater Arab Free Trade Area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Arab_Free_Trade_Area "Greater Arab Free Trade Area"),[\[216\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-223)\[*[unreliable source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Reliable_sources "Wikipedia:Reliable sources")*] the [African Continental Free Trade Area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African_Continental_Free_Trade_Area "African Continental Free Trade Area"),[\[217\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-224) and has an association agreement with the European Union.[\[218\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-225)[\[219\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-226)

Turkish direct investments have accelerated in Algeria, with total value reaching $5 billion. As of 2022, the number of Turkish companies present in Algeria has reached 1,400. In 2020, despite the pandemic, more than 130 Turkish companies were created in Algeria.[\[220\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-227)

### Oil and natural resources

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Algeria\&action=edit\&section=19 "Edit section: Oil and natural resources")]

See also: [Mining industry of Algeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mining_industry_of_Algeria "Mining industry of Algeria")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a9/Algeria_pipelines_map.jpg/220px-Algeria_pipelines_map.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Algeria_pipelines_map.jpg)

Pipelines across Algeria

Algeria, whose economy is reliant on petroleum, has been an [OPEC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OPEC "OPEC") member since 1969. Its crude oil production stands at around 1.1 million barrels/day, but it is also a major gas producer and exporter, with important links to Europe.[\[221\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-opb15-228) Hydrocarbons have long been the backbone of the economy, accounting for roughly 60% of budget revenues, 30% of GDP, and 87.7%[\[222\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-229) of export earnings. Algeria has the [10th-largest reserves of natural gas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_natural_gas_proven_reserves "List of countries by natural gas proven reserves") in the world and is the [sixth-largest gas exporter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_natural_gas_exports "List of countries by natural gas exports"). The U.S. [Energy Information Administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_Information_Administration "Energy Information Administration") reported that in 2005, Algeria had 4.5 trillion cubic metres (160×10~~^~~12 cu ft) of proven [natural gas reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_gas_reserves "Natural gas reserves").[\[223\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-ciawfb-230) It also ranks [16th in oil reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_proven_oil_reserves "List of countries by proven oil reserves").[\[15\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-AlgeriaFactbook-21)

Non-hydrocarbon growth for 2011 was projected at 5%. To cope with social demands, the authorities raised expenditure, especially on basic food support, employment creation, support for SMEs, and higher salaries. High hydrocarbon prices have improved the current account and the already large international reserves position.[\[215\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-imfart4-222)

Income from oil and gas rose in 2011 as a result of continuing high oil prices, though the trend in production volume is downward.[\[161\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-aaeo-168) Production from the oil and gas sector in terms of volume continues to decline, dropping from 43.2 million tonnes to 32 million tonnes between 2007 and 2011. Nevertheless, the sector accounted for 98% of the total volume of exports in 2011, against 48% in 1962,[\[224\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-231) and 70% of budgetary receipts, or US$71.4 billion.[\[161\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-aaeo-168)

The Algerian national oil company is [Sonatrach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sonatrach "Sonatrach"), which plays a key role in all aspects of the oil and natural gas sectors in Algeria. All foreign operators must work in partnership with Sonatrach, which usually has majority ownership in production-sharing agreements.[\[225\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-232)

Access to [biocapacity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biocapacity "Biocapacity") in Algeria is lower than world average. In 2016, Algeria had 0.53 global hectares[\[226\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-footprintdata-233) of biocapacity per person within its territory, much less than the world average of 1.6 global hectares per person.[\[227\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-234) In 2016, Algeria used 2.4 global hectares of biocapacity per person – their [ecological footprint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecological_footprint "Ecological footprint") of consumption. This means they use just under 4.5 times as much biocapacity as Algeria contains. As a result, Algeria is running a biocapacity deficit.[\[226\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-footprintdata-233) In April 2022, diplomats from Italy and Spain held talks after Rome's move to secure large volume of Algerian gas stoked concerns in Madrid.[\[228\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-235) Under the deal between Algeria's Sonatrach and Italy's Eni, Algeria will send an additional 9 billion cubic metres of gas to Italy by next year and in 2024.[\[229\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-236)

### Research and alternative energy sources

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Algeria\&action=edit\&section=20 "Edit section: Research and alternative energy sources")]

Algeria has invested an estimated 100 billion dinars towards developing research facilities and paying researchers. This development programme is meant to advance alternative energy production, especially solar and wind power.[\[230\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-237) Algeria is estimated to have the largest solar energy potential in the Mediterranean, so the government has funded the creation of a solar science park in Hassi R'Mel. Currently, Algeria has 20,000 research professors at various universities and over 780 research labs, with state-set goals to expand to 1,000. Besides solar energy, areas of research in Algeria include space and satellite telecommunications, nuclear power and medical research.

### Labour market

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Algeria\&action=edit\&section=21 "Edit section: Labour market")]

The overall rate of unemployment was 11.8% in 2023.[\[231\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-unemplyment-238) The government strengthened in 2011 the job programmes introduced in 1988, in particular in the framework of the programme to aid those seeking work (Dispositif d'Aide à l'Insertion Professionnelle).[\[161\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-aaeo-168)

Despite a decline in total unemployment, youth and women unemployment is high.[\[215\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-imfart4-222)

### Tourism

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Algeria\&action=edit\&section=22 "Edit section: Tourism")]

Main article: [Tourism in Algeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_Algeria "Tourism in Algeria")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7d/Giants_-_Rock_towers_in_the_Algerian_Sahara.jpg/220px-Giants_-_Rock_towers_in_the_Algerian_Sahara.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Giants_-_Rock_towers_in_the_Algerian_Sahara.jpg)

[Djanet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Djanet "Djanet")

The development of the tourism sector in Algeria had previously been hampered by a lack of facilities, but since 2004 a broad tourism development strategy has been implemented resulting in many hotels of a high modern standard being built.

There are several [UNESCO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNESCO "UNESCO") [World Heritage Sites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Heritage_Sites "World Heritage Sites") in Algeria[\[232\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-239) which includes [Al Qal'a of Beni Hammad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Qal%27a_of_Beni_Hammad "Al Qal'a of Beni Hammad"), the first capital of the [Hammadid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hammadid "Hammadid") empire; [Tipasa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tipasa "Tipasa"), a Phoenician and later Roman town;[Djémila](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dj%C3%A9mila "Djémila") and [Timgad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timgad "Timgad"), both [Roman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Rome "Ancient Rome") ruins; [M'Zab Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%27Zab_Valley "M'Zab Valley"), a limestone valley containing a large urbanised [oasis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oasis "Oasis"); and the [Casbah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casbah "Casbah") of Algiers, an important citadel. The only natural [World Heritage Site](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Heritage_Site "World Heritage Site") in Algeria is the [Tassili n'Ajjer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tassili_n%27Ajjer "Tassili n'Ajjer"), a mountain range.

### Transport

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Algeria\&action=edit\&section=23 "Edit section: Transport")]

Main article: [Transport in Algeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_in_Algeria "Transport in Algeria")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/28/Autoroute_est_ouest_ghomri2.JPG/220px-Autoroute_est_ouest_ghomri2.JPG)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Autoroute_est_ouest_ghomri2.JPG)

The main highway connecting the Moroccan to the Tunisian border was a part of the [Cairo–Dakar Highway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cairo%E2%80%93Dakar_Highway "Cairo–Dakar Highway") project

Two trans-African automobile routes pass through Algeria:

- [Cairo-Dakar Highway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cairo-Dakar_Highway "Cairo-Dakar Highway")
- [Algiers-Lagos Highway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algiers-Lagos_Highway "Algiers-Lagos Highway")

The Algerian road network is the densest in Africa; its length is estimated at 180,000 km (110,000 mi) of highways, with more than 3,756 structures and a paving rate of 85%. This network will be complemented by the [East-West Highway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algeria_East%E2%80%93West_Highway "Algeria East–West Highway"), a major infrastructure project currently under construction. It is a three-way, 1,216-kilometre-long (756 mi) highway, linking [Annaba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annaba "Annaba") in the extreme east to the [Tlemcen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tlemcen "Tlemcen") in the far west. Algeria is also crossed by the [Trans-Sahara Highway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trans-Sahara_Highway "Trans-Sahara Highway"), which is now completely paved. This road is supported by the Algerian government to increase trade between the six countries crossed: Algeria, [Mali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mali "Mali"), [Niger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Niger "Niger"), [Nigeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nigeria "Nigeria"), [Chad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chad "Chad"), and [Tunisia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tunisia "Tunisia").

## Demographics

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Algeria\&action=edit\&section=24 "Edit section: Demographics")]

Main article: [Demographics of Algeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_Algeria "Demographics of Algeria")

See also: [List of cities in Algeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_in_Algeria "List of cities in Algeria")

Algeria has a population of an estimated 45.6 million,[\[233\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-240) of which the majority, 75%[\[234\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-EB-2022-241) to 85% are ethnically [Arab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabs "Arabs").[\[15\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-AlgeriaFactbook-21)[\[235\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-ons-242)[\[236\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-ber_evidance-243) At the outset of the 20th century, its population was approximately 4 million.[\[237\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-244) About 90% of Algerians live in the northern, coastal area; the inhabitants of the Sahara desert are mainly concentrated in [oases](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oasis "Oasis"), although some 1.5 million remain [nomadic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nomad "Nomad") or partly nomadic. 28.1% of Algerians are under the age of 15.[\[15\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-AlgeriaFactbook-21)

Between 90,000 and 165,000 [Sahrawis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sahrawi_people "Sahrawi people") from [Western Sahara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Sahara "Western Sahara") live in the [Sahrawi refugee camps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sahrawi_refugee_camps "Sahrawi refugee camps"),[\[238\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-UNHCRAlgeria-245)[\[239\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-USCRIAlgeria-246) in the western Algerian Sahara desert.[\[240\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-247) There are also more than 4,000 [Palestinian refugees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_refugee "Palestinian refugee"), who are well integrated and have not asked for assistance from the United Nations High Commissioner for Refugees (UNHCR).[\[238\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-UNHCRAlgeria-245)[\[239\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-USCRIAlgeria-246) In 2009, 35,000 [Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Overseas_Chinese "Overseas Chinese") migrant workers lived in Algeria.[\[241\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-248)

The largest concentration of Algerian migrants outside Algeria is in France, which has reportedly over 1.7 million [Algerians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algerians "Algerians") of up to the second generation.[\[242\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-249)
