### Ethnic groups

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Algeria\&action=edit\&section=25 "Edit section: Ethnic groups")]

Main article: [Ethnic groups in Algeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnic_groups_in_Algeria "Ethnic groups in Algeria")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d2/Tenus_traditionnelles_alg%C3%A9riennes_27.png/220px-Tenus_traditionnelles_alg%C3%A9riennes_27.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Tenus_traditionnelles_alg%C3%A9riennes_27.png)

Some of Algeria's traditional clothes

[Arabs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabs "Arabs") and indigenous [Berbers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berbers "Berbers") as well as [Phoenicians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phoenicians "Phoenicians"), [Romans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Rome "Ancient Rome"), [Vandals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vandals "Vandals"), [Byzantine Greeks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Byzantine_Greeks "Byzantine Greeks"), [Turks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_people "Turkish people"), various [Sub-Saharan Africans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sub-Saharan_Africans "Sub-Saharan Africans"), and French have contributed to the history and culture of Algeria.[\[244\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-251) Descendants of [Andalusi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Andalus "Al-Andalus") refugees are also present in the population of Algiers and other cities.[\[245\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-252) Moreover, Spanish was spoken by these [Aragonese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aragon "Aragon") and [Castillian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Castilian_people "Castilian people") [Morisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moriscos "Moriscos") descendants deep into the 18th century, and even [Catalan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catalan_language "Catalan language") was spoken at the same time by [Catalan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catalonia "Catalonia") [Morisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moriscos "Moriscos") descendants in the small town of Grish El-Oued.[\[246\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-253)

Centuries of [Arab migrations to the Maghreb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_migrations_to_the_Maghreb "Arab migrations to the Maghreb") since the seventh century shifted the demographic scope in Algeria. Estimates vary based on different sources. The majority of the population of Algeria is ethnically [Arab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabs "Arabs"), constituting between 75%[\[234\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-EB-2022-241)[\[247\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-DK-2016-254)[\[248\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Seddon-2013-255)[\[249\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-DK-2005-256) and 80%[\[250\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-ESU-2024-257)[\[251\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Laaredj-Campbell-2015-258)[\[252\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-259) to 85%[\[253\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-260)[\[254\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-261) of the population. [Berbers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berbers "Berbers") who make up between 15%[\[15\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-AlgeriaFactbook-21) and 20%[\[251\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Laaredj-Campbell-2015-258)[\[250\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-ESU-2024-257)[\[255\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-262) to 24%[\[247\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-DK-2016-254)[\[248\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Seddon-2013-255)[\[249\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-DK-2005-256) of the population are divided into many groups with varying languages. The largest of these are the [Kabyles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kabyle_people "Kabyle people"), who live in the [Kabylie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kabylie "Kabylie") region east of Algiers, the [Chaoui](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chaoui_people "Chaoui people") of Northeast Algeria, the [Tuaregs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tuareg_people "Tuareg people") in the southern desert and the [Shenwa people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chenouas "Chenouas") of North Algeria.[\[256\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-263)\[*[page needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citing_sources "Wikipedia:Citing sources")*] During the colonial period, there was a large (10% in 1960)[\[257\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Cook-264) [European](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnic_groups_in_Europe "Ethnic groups in Europe") population who became known as *[Pied-Noirs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pied-Noir "Pied-Noir")*. They were primarily of French, Spanish and [Italian origin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Algerians "Italian Algerians"). Almost all of this population left during the war of independence or immediately after its end.[\[258\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-265)

### Languages

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Algeria\&action=edit\&section=26 "Edit section: Languages")]

Main article: [Languages of Algeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Languages_of_Algeria "Languages of Algeria")

[Modern Standard Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modern_Standard_Arabic "Modern Standard Arabic") and [Berber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berber_language "Berber language") are the official languages.[\[259\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Constitution_of_Algeria-266) [Algerian Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algerian_Arabic "Algerian Arabic") (Darja) is the language used by the majority of the population. Colloquial Algerian Arabic has some Berber loanwords which represent 8% to 9% of its vocabulary.[\[260\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Wexler-2012-267)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/45/Tizi_Ouzou_Tasdawit.jpg/220px-Tizi_Ouzou_Tasdawit.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Tizi_Ouzou_Tasdawit.jpg)

Signs in the [University of Tizi Ouzou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Tizi_Ouzou "University of Tizi Ouzou") in three languages: [Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic "Arabic"), [Berber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berber_languages "Berber languages"), and French

[Berber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berber_language "Berber language") has been recognised as a "national language" by the constitutional amendment of 8 May 2002.[\[261\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-268) [Kabyle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kabyle_language "Kabyle language"), the predominant Berber language, is taught and is partially co-official (with a few restrictions) in parts of [Kabylie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kabylie "Kabylie"). Kabyle has a significant [Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic "Arabic"), [French](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_language "French language"), [Latin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin "Latin"), [Greek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greek_language "Greek language"), [Phoenician](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phoenician_language "Phoenician language") and [Punic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Punic_language "Punic language") substratum, and Arabic loanwords represent 35% of the total Kabyle vocabulary.[\[262\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-269) In February 2016, the Algerian constitution passed a resolution that made Berber an official language alongside Arabic. Algeria emerged as a bilingual state after 1962.[\[263\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-270) Colloquial [Algerian Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algerian_Arabic "Algerian Arabic") is spoken by about 83% of the population and Berber by 27%.[\[264\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-leclerc-271)

Although French has no official status in Algeria, it has one of the largest Francophone populations in the world,[\[265\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-272) and French is widely used in government, media (newspapers, radio, local television), and both the education system (from primary school onwards) and academia due to [Algeria's colonial history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_rule_in_Algeria "French rule in Algeria"). It can be regarded as a [lingua franca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lingua_franca "Lingua franca") of Algeria. In 2008, 11.2 million Algerians could read and write in French.[\[266\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-273) In 2013, it was estimated that 60% of the population could speak or understand French.[\[267\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Natalie_Edwards-274) In 2022, it was estimated that 33% of the population was Francophone.[\[268\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-275)

The use of [English in Algeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_in_Algeria "English in Algeria"), though limited in comparison to the previously mentioned languages, has increased due to globalisation.[\[269\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Maraf_Osam_2023_pp._307%E2%80%93314-276)[\[270\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Allah_2023_k545-277) In 2022 it was announced that English would be taught in elementary schools.[\[271\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-The_Associated_Press_2024_o507-278)

### Religion

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Algeria\&action=edit\&section=27 "Edit section: Religion")]

Main article: [Religion in Algeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion_in_Algeria "Religion in Algeria")

See also: [Islam in Algeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_in_Algeria "Islam in Algeria"), [Early African Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Early_African_Church "Early African Church"), and [History of the Jews in Algeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Jews_in_Algeria "History of the Jews in Algeria")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/86/Mosque_Hassan_Pacha_Oran_1790_%286%29.jpg/220px-Mosque_Hassan_Pacha_Oran_1790_%286%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Mosque_Hassan_Pacha_Oran_1790_\(6\).jpg)

[Hassan Pasha Mosque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hassan_Pasha_Mosque "Hassan Pasha Mosque") in [Oran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oran "Oran")

[Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam "Islam") is the predominant religion in Algeria, with its adherents, mostly Sunnis, accounting for 99% of the population according to a 2021 CIA *[World Factbook](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Factbook "World Factbook")* estimate,[\[15\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-AlgeriaFactbook-21) and 97.9% according to Pew Research in 2020.[\[272\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-pew-279) There are about 290,000 [Ibadis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ibadi "Ibadi") in the M'zab Valley in the region of [Ghardaia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghardaia "Ghardaia").

Prior to independence, Algeria was home to more than 1.3 million Christians (mostly of [European ancestry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pied-Noir "Pied-Noir")).[\[273\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-280) Most of the Christian settlers left to France after the country's independence.[\[274\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-281)[\[275\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-282) Today, estimates of the [Christian population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianity_in_Algeria "Christianity in Algeria") range from 100,000 to 200,000.[\[276\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-US_DoS-2020-283) Algerian citizens who are Christians predominantly belong to [Protestant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestantism "Protestantism") denominations, which have seen increased pressure from the government in recent years including many forced closures.[\[276\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-US_DoS-2020-283)

According to the [Arab Barometer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Barometer "Arab Barometer") in 2018–2019, the vast majority of Algerians (99.1%) continue to identify as Muslim.[\[277\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Arab_Barometer-2023-284) The June 2019 [Arab Barometer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Barometer "Arab Barometer")-[BBC News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BBC_News "BBC News") report found that the percentage of Algerians identifying as non-religious has grown from around 8% in 2013 to around 15% in 2018.[\[278\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-285) The Arab Barometer December 2019, found that the growth in the percentage of Algerians identifying as non-religious is largely driven by young Algerians, with roughly 25% describing themselves as non-religious.[\[279\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-286) However, the 2021 Arab Barometer report found that those who said they were not religious among Algerians has decreased, with just 2.6% identifying as non-religious. In that same report, 69.5% of Algerians identified as religious and another 27.8% identifying as somewhat religious.[\[277\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Arab_Barometer-2023-284)[\[280\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-287)

Algeria has given the [Muslim world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muslim_world "Muslim world") a number of prominent thinkers, including [Emir Abdelkader](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emir_Abdelkader "Emir Abdelkader"), [Abdelhamid Ben Badis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdelhamid_Ben_Badis "Abdelhamid Ben Badis"), [Mouloud Kacem Naît Belkacem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mouloud_Kacem_Na%C3%AEt_Belkacem "Mouloud Kacem Naît Belkacem"), [Malek Bennabi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malek_Bennabi "Malek Bennabi") and [Mohamed Arkoun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohamed_Arkoun "Mohamed Arkoun").

### Health

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Algeria\&action=edit\&section=28 "Edit section: Health")]

Main article: [Health in Algeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_in_Algeria "Health in Algeria")

In 2018, Algeria had the highest numbers of physicians in the Maghreb region (1.72 per 1,000 people), nurses (2.23 per 1,000 people), and dentists (0.31 per 1,000 people). Access to "improved water sources" was around 97.4% of the population in urban areas and 98.7% of the population in the rural areas. Some 99% of Algerians living in urban areas, and around 93.4% of those living in rural areas, had access to "improved sanitation". According to the World Bank, Algeria is making progress towards its goal of "reducing by half the number of people without sustainable access to improved drinking water and basic sanitation by 2015". Given Algeria's young population, policy favours preventive health care and clinics over hospitals. In keeping with this policy, the government maintains an immunisation programme. However, poor sanitation and unclean water still cause [tuberculosis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tuberculosis "Tuberculosis"), [hepatitis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hepatitis "Hepatitis"), [measles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Measles "Measles"), [typhoid fever](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Typhoid_fever "Typhoid fever"), [cholera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cholera "Cholera") and [dysentery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dysentery "Dysentery"). The poor generally receive healthcare free of charge.[\[281\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-288)

Health records have been maintained in Algeria since 1882 and began adding Muslims living in the south to their vital record database in 1905 during French rule.[\[282\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-289)

### Education

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Algeria\&action=edit\&section=29 "Edit section: Education")]

Main articles: [Education in Algeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_Algeria "Education in Algeria") and [List of universities in Algeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_universities_in_Algeria "List of universities in Algeria")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/14/UIS_Literacy_Rate_Algeria_population_plus15_1980_2015.png/220px-UIS_Literacy_Rate_Algeria_population_plus15_1980_2015.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:UIS_Literacy_Rate_Algeria_population_plus15_1980_2015.png)

[UIS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNESCO_Institute_for_Statistics "UNESCO Institute for Statistics") literacy rate Algeria population plus 15 1985–2015

Since the 1970s, in a centralised system that was designed to significantly reduce the rate of illiteracy, the Algerian government introduced a decree by which school attendance became compulsory for all children aged between 6 and 15 years who have the ability to track their learning through the 20 facilities built since independence, now the literacy rate is around 92.6%.[\[283\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-APSLIT-290) Since 1972, Arabic is used as the language of instruction during the first nine years of schooling. From the third year, French is taught and it is also the language of instruction for science classes. The students can also learn English, Italian, Spanish and German. In 2008, new programmes at the elementary appeared, therefore the compulsory schooling does not start at the age of six anymore, but at the age of five.[\[284\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-291) Apart from the 122 private schools, the Universities of the State are free of charge. After nine years of primary school, students can go to a high school or to an educational institution. The school offers two programmes: general or technical. At the end of the third year of secondary school, students pass the exam of the baccalaureate, which allows once it is successful to pursue graduate studies in universities and institutes.[\[285\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-292)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/23/Wikipedia_Education_Program_Algeria_V2_Ceremony_%2896%29.jpg/220px-Wikipedia_Education_Program_Algeria_V2_Ceremony_%2896%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Wikipedia_Education_Program_Algeria_V2_Ceremony_\(96\).jpg)

Students at the [University of Chlef](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Chlef "University of Chlef") in Algeria

Education is officially compulsory for children between the ages of six and 15. In 2008, the illiteracy rate for people over 10 was 22.3%, 15.6% for men and 29.0% for women. The province with the lowest rate of illiteracy was [Algiers Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algiers_Province "Algiers Province") at 11.6%, while the province with the highest rate was [Djelfa Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Djelfa_Province "Djelfa Province") at 35.5%.[\[286\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-illit-293)

Algeria has 26 universities and 67 institutions of higher education, which must accommodate a million Algerians and 80,000 foreign students in 2008. The [University of Algiers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Algiers "University of Algiers"), founded in 1879, is the oldest, it offers education in various disciplines (law, medicine, science and letters). Twenty-five of these universities and almost all of the institutions of higher education were founded after the independence of the country.

Even if some of them offer instruction in [Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic "Arabic") like areas of law and the economy, most of the other sectors such as science and medicine continue to be provided in French and English. Among the most important universities, there are the [University of Sciences and Technology Houari Boumediene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Sciences_and_Technology_Houari_Boumediene "University of Sciences and Technology Houari Boumediene"), the [University of Mentouri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Mentouri "University of Mentouri") Constantine, and [University of Oran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Oran "University of Oran") Es-Senia. The [University of Abou Bekr Belkaïd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Abou_Bekr_Belka%C3%AFd "University of Abou Bekr Belkaïd") in Tlemcen and [University of Batna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Batna "University of Batna") Hadj Lakhdar occupy the 26th and 45th row in Africa.[\[287\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-294) Algeria was ranked 115th in the [Global Innovation Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Innovation_Index "Global Innovation Index") in 2024.[\[288\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-295)

## Culture

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Algeria\&action=edit\&section=30 "Edit section: Culture")]

Main article: [Culture of Algeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Algeria "Culture of Algeria")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/ce/Bensari2.jpg/220px-Bensari2.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Bensari2.jpg)

Algerian musicians in [Tlemcen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tlemcen "Tlemcen"), [Regency of Algiers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regency_of_Algiers "Regency of Algiers"); by [Bachir Yellès](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bachir_Yell%C3%A8s "Bachir Yellès")

Modern Algerian literature, split between [Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classical_Arabic "Classical Arabic"), [Tamazight](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berber_languages "Berber languages") and French, has been strongly influenced by the country's recent history. [Famous novelists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Algerian_writers "List of Algerian writers") of the 20th century include [Mohammed Dib](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammed_Dib "Mohammed Dib"), [Albert Camus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albert_Camus "Albert Camus"), [Kateb Yacine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kateb_Yacine "Kateb Yacine") and [Ahlam Mosteghanemi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahlam_Mosteghanemi "Ahlam Mosteghanemi") while [Assia Djebar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assia_Djebar "Assia Djebar") is widely translated. Among the important novelists of the 1980s were [Rachid Mimouni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rachid_Mimouni "Rachid Mimouni"), later vice-president of [Amnesty International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amnesty_International "Amnesty International"), and [Tahar Djaout](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tahar_Djaout "Tahar Djaout"), murdered by an [Islamist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamist "Islamist") group in 1993 for his secularist views.[\[289\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-296)

[Malek Bennabi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malek_Bennabi "Malek Bennabi") and [Frantz Fanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frantz_Fanon "Frantz Fanon") are noted for their thoughts on [decolonisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Decolonization "Decolonization"); [Augustine of Hippo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Augustine_of_Hippo "Augustine of Hippo") was born in [Tagaste](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tagaste "Tagaste") (modern-day [Souk Ahras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Souk_Ahras "Souk Ahras")); and [Ibn Khaldun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ibn_Khaldun "Ibn Khaldun"), though born in [Tunis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tunis "Tunis"), wrote the [Muqaddima](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muqaddima "Muqaddima") while staying in Algeria. The works of the [Sanusi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senussi "Senussi") family in pre-colonial times, and of [Emir Abdelkader](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emir_Abdelkader "Emir Abdelkader") and Sheikh [Ben Badis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdelhamid_Ben_Badis "Abdelhamid Ben Badis") in colonial times, are widely noted. The Latin author [Apuleius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apuleius "Apuleius") was born in [Madaurus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madaurus "Madaurus") (Mdaourouch), in what later became Algeria.

Contemporary [Algerian cinema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algerian_cinema "Algerian cinema") is varied in terms of genre, exploring a wider range of themes and issues. There has been a transition from cinema which focused on the war of independence to films more concerned with the everyday lives of Algerians.[\[290\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-297)

### Media

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Algeria\&action=edit\&section=31 "Edit section: Media")]

Main article: [Media of Algeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_of_Algeria "Media of Algeria")

### Art

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Algeria\&action=edit\&section=32 "Edit section: Art")]

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3a/Portrait-racim.jpg/140px-Portrait-racim.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Portrait-racim.jpg)

[Mohammed Racim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammed_Racim "Mohammed Racim"); founder of the Algerian school for painting

Algerian painters, like [Mohammed Racim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammed_Racim "Mohammed Racim") and [Baya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baya_\(artist\) "Baya (artist)"), attempted to revive the prestigious Algerian past prior to French colonisation, at the same time that they have contributed to the preservation of the authentic values of Algeria. In this line, [Mohamed Temam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohamed_Temam "Mohamed Temam"), [Abdelkhader Houamel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdelkhader_Houamel "Abdelkhader Houamel") have also returned through this art, scenes from the history of the country, the habits and customs of the past and the country life. Other new artistic currents including the one of [M'hamed Issiakhem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%27hamed_Issiakhem "M'hamed Issiakhem"), [Mohammed Khadda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammed_Khadda "Mohammed Khadda") and [Bachir Yelles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bachir_Yelles "Bachir Yelles"), appeared on the scene of Algerian painting, abandoning figurative classical painting to find new pictorial ways, to adapt Algerian paintings to the new realities of the country through its struggle and its aspirations. [Mohammed Khadda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammed_Khadda "Mohammed Khadda")[\[291\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-art-298) and [M'hamed Issiakhem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%27hamed_Issiakhem "M'hamed Issiakhem") have been notable in recent years.[\[291\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-art-298)

### Literature

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Algeria\&action=edit\&section=33 "Edit section: Literature")]

|                                                                                                                                                                            |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |

| [![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/50px-Question_book-new.svg.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Question_book-new.svg) | This section **does not [cite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citing_sources "Wikipedia:Citing sources") any [sources](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability "Wikipedia:Verifiability")**. Please help [improve this section](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special:EditPage/Algeria "Special:EditPage/Algeria") by [adding citations to reliable sources](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Referencing_for_beginners "Help:Referencing for beginners"). Unsourced material may be challenged and [removed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability#Burden_of_evidence "Wikipedia:Verifiability"). *Find sources:* ["Algeria"](https://www.google.com/search?as_eq=wikipedia\&q=%22Algeria%22) – [news](https://www.google.com/search?tbm=nws\&q=%22Algeria%22+-wikipedia\&tbs=ar:1) **·** [newspapers](https://www.google.com/search?\&q=%22Algeria%22\&tbs=bkt:s\&tbm=bks) **·** [books](https://www.google.com/search?tbs=bks:1\&q=%22Algeria%22+-wikipedia) **·** [scholar](https://scholar.google.com/scholar?q=%22Algeria%22) **·** [JSTOR](https://www.jstor.org/action/doBasicSearch?Query=%22Algeria%22\&acc=on\&wc=on) *(September 2024)**&#x20;([Learn how and when to remove this message](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Maintenance_template_removal "Help:Maintenance template removal"))* |

Main articles: [Algerian literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algerian_literature "Algerian literature") and [List of Algerian writers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Algerian_writers "List of Algerian writers")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e6/Lucius_Apuleius_Platonicus%2C_from_%27Crabbes_Historical_Dictionary%27%2C_published_in_1825_%28C19%29.jpg/114px-Lucius_Apuleius_Platonicus%2C_from_%27Crabbes_Historical_Dictionary%27%2C_published_in_1825_%28C19%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Lucius_Apuleius_Platonicus,_from_%27Crabbes_Historical_Dictionary%27,_published_in_1825_\(C19\).jpg)

[Apuleius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apuleius "Apuleius")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c0/Kateb_Yacine_Nedjma_authograph.jpg/120px-Kateb_Yacine_Nedjma_authograph.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Kateb_Yacine_Nedjma_authograph.jpg)

[Kateb Yacine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kateb_Yacine "Kateb Yacine")

The historic roots of Algerian literature go back to the [Numidian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Numidia "Numidia") and [Roman African](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Africa_\(Roman_province\) "Africa (Roman province)") era, when [Apuleius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apuleius "Apuleius") wrote *[The Golden Ass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Golden_Ass "The Golden Ass")*, the only Latin novel to survive in its entirety. This period had also known [Augustine of Hippo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Augustine_of_Hippo "Augustine of Hippo"), [Nonius Marcellus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nonius_Marcellus "Nonius Marcellus") and [Martianus Capella](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martianus_Capella "Martianus Capella"), among many others. The Middle Ages have known many Arabic writers who revolutionised the Arab world literature, with authors like [Ahmad al-Buni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmad_al-Buni "Ahmad al-Buni"), [Ibn Manzur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ibn_Manzur "Ibn Manzur") and [Ibn Khaldoun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ibn_Khaldoun "Ibn Khaldoun"), who wrote the [Muqaddimah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muqaddimah "Muqaddimah") while staying in Algeria, and many others.

[Albert Camus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albert_Camus "Albert Camus") was an Algerian-born French Pied-Noir author. In 1957, he was awarded the [Nobel Prize in Literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nobel_Prize_in_Literature "Nobel Prize in Literature").

Today Algeria contains, in its literary landscape, big names having not only marked the Algerian literature, but also the universal literary heritage in Arabic and French.

As a first step, Algerian literature was marked by works whose main concern was the assertion of the Algerian national entity, there is the publication of novels as the *Algerian trilogy* of [Mohammed Dib](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammed_Dib "Mohammed Dib"), or even *Nedjma* of [Kateb Yacine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kateb_Yacine "Kateb Yacine") novel which is often regarded as a monumental and major work. Other known writers will contribute to the emergence of Algerian literature whom include [Mouloud Feraoun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mouloud_Feraoun "Mouloud Feraoun"), [Malek Bennabi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malek_Bennabi "Malek Bennabi"), [Malek Haddad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malek_Haddad "Malek Haddad"), [Moufdi Zakaria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moufdi_Zakaria "Moufdi Zakaria"), Abdelhamid Ben Badis, Mohamed Laïd Al-Khalifa, [Mouloud Mammeri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mouloud_Mammeri "Mouloud Mammeri"), [Frantz Fanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frantz_Fanon "Frantz Fanon"), and [Assia Djebar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assia_Djebar "Assia Djebar").

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ad/Ahlem_Mosteghanemi_at_Beirut_Book_Fair_2012.JPG/170px-Ahlem_Mosteghanemi_at_Beirut_Book_Fair_2012.JPG)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Ahlem_Mosteghanemi_at_Beirut_Book_Fair_2012.JPG)

[Ahlam Mosteghanemi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahlam_Mosteghanemi "Ahlam Mosteghanemi"), the most widely read female writer in the [Arab world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_world "Arab world").[\[292\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-299)

In the aftermath of the independence, several new authors emerged on the Algerian literary scene, they will attempt through their works to expose a number of social problems, among them there are [Rachid Boudjedra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rachid_Boudjedra "Rachid Boudjedra"), [Rachid Mimouni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rachid_Mimouni "Rachid Mimouni"), [Leila Sebbar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leila_Sebbar "Leila Sebbar"), [Tahar Djaout](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tahar_Djaout "Tahar Djaout") and [Tahir Wattar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tahir_Wattar "Tahir Wattar").

Currently, a part of Algerian writers tends to be defined in a literature of shocking expression, due to the terrorism that occurred during the 1990s, the other party is defined in a different style of literature who staged an individualistic conception of the human adventure. Among the most noted recent works, there is the writer, *the swallows of Kabul* and *the attack* of [Yasmina Khadra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yasmina_Khadra "Yasmina Khadra"), *the oath of barbarians* of [Boualem Sansal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boualem_Sansal "Boualem Sansal"), *memory of the flesh* of [Ahlam Mosteghanemi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahlam_Mosteghanemi "Ahlam Mosteghanemi") and the last novel by Assia Djebar *nowhere in my father's House*.

### Cinema

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Algeria\&action=edit\&section=34 "Edit section: Cinema")]

Main article: [Cinema of Algeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinema_of_Algeria "Cinema of Algeria")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e2/Mohamed_LAKHDAR_HAMINA.png/220px-Mohamed_LAKHDAR_HAMINA.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Mohamed_LAKHDAR_HAMINA.png)

[Mohammed Lakhdar-Hamina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammed_Lakhdar-Hamina "Mohammed Lakhdar-Hamina"), one of the most prominent figures in contemporary Arabic cinema.

The Algerian state's interest in film-industry activities can be seen in the annual budget of DZD 200 million (EUR 1.3 million) allocated to production, specific measures and an ambitious programme plan implemented by the Ministry of Culture to promote national production, renovate the cinema stock and remedy the weak links in distribution and exploitation.

The financial support provided by the state, through the Fund for the Development of the Arts, Techniques and the Film Industry (FDATIC) and the Algerian Agency for Cultural Influence (AARC), plays a key role in the promotion of national production. Between 2007 and 2013, FDATIC subsidised 98 films (feature films, documentaries and short films). In mid-2013, AARC had already supported a total of 78 films, including 42 feature films, 6 short films and 30 documentaries.

According to the European Audiovisual Observatory's LUMIERE database, 41 Algerian films were distributed in Europe between 1996 and 2013; 21 films in this repertoire were Algerian-French co-productions. [*Days of Glory* (2006)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Days_of_Glory_\(2006_film\) "Days of Glory (2006 film)") and [*Outside the Law* (2010)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Outside_the_Law_\(2010_film\) "Outside the Law (2010 film)") recorded the highest number of admissions in the European Union, 3,172,612 and 474,722, respectively.[\[293\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-300)

Algeria won the [Palme d'Or](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palme_d%27Or "Palme d'Or") for *[Chronicle of the Years of Fire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chronicle_of_the_Years_of_Fire "Chronicle of the Years of Fire")* (1975), two Oscars for [*Z* (1969)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Z_\(1969_film\) "Z (1969 film)"), and other awards for the Italian-Algerian movie *[The Battle of Algiers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Battle_of_Algiers "The Battle of Algiers")*.

### Cuisine

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Algeria\&action=edit\&section=35 "Edit section: Cuisine")]

Main article: [Algerian cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algerian_cuisine "Algerian cuisine")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3c/Couscous_%28Alg%C3%A9rie%2C_lieu_exact_non_pr%C3%A9cis%C3%A9%29.jpg/150px-Couscous_%28Alg%C3%A9rie%2C_lieu_exact_non_pr%C3%A9cis%C3%A9%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Couscous_\(Alg%C3%A9rie,_lieu_exact_non_pr%C3%A9cis%C3%A9\).jpg)

[Couscous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Couscous "Couscous"), the national dish of Algeria

Algerian cuisine is rich and diverse as a result of interactions and exchanges with other cultures and nations over the centuries.[\[294\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Encyclopedia_Britannica_1998_v327-301) It is based on both land and sea products. Conquests or demographic movement towards the Algerian territory were two of the main factors of exchanges between the different peoples and cultures. The Algerian cuisine is a mix of [Arab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_cuisine "Arab cuisine"), [Berber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berber_cuisine "Berber cuisine"), [Turkish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_cuisine "Turkish cuisine") and [French](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_cuisine "French cuisine") roots.[\[295\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-302)[\[294\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Encyclopedia_Britannica_1998_v327-301)

Algerian cuisine offers a variety of dishes depending on the region and the season, but vegetables and [cereals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cereal "Cereal") remain at its core. Most of the Algerian dishes are centered around bread, meats (lamb, beef or poultry), olive oil, vegetables, and fresh herbs. Vegetables are often used for salads, soups, [tajines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tajine "Tajine"), [couscous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Couscous "Couscous"), and sauce-based dishes. Of all the Algerian traditional dishes available, the most famous one is [couscous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algerian_couscous "Algerian couscous"), recognized as a national dish.[\[296\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-ebin.pub_2005_h182-303)

### Sports

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Algeria\&action=edit\&section=36 "Edit section: Sports")]

Main article: [Sport in Algeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sport_in_Algeria "Sport in Algeria")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5b/Alg%C3%A9rie_-_Arm%C3%A9nie_-_20140531_-_Equipe_d%27Alg%C3%A9rie.jpg/220px-Alg%C3%A9rie_-_Arm%C3%A9nie_-_20140531_-_Equipe_d%27Alg%C3%A9rie.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Alg%C3%A9rie_-_Arm%C3%A9nie_-_20140531_-_Equipe_d%27Alg%C3%A9rie.jpg)

The [Algeria national football team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algeria_national_football_team "Algeria national football team")

Various games have existed in Algeria since antiquity. In the [Aures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aur%C3%A8s_Mountains "Aurès Mountains"), people played several games such as El Kherba or El khergueba ([chess](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chess "Chess") variant). Playing cards, [checkers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Checkers "Checkers") and chess games are part of Algerian culture. Racing ([fantasia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fantasia_\(culture\) "Fantasia (culture)")) and [rifle shooting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rifle_shooting "Rifle shooting") are part of cultural recreation of the Algerians.[\[297\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-304)

[Football](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_in_Algeria "Football in Algeria") is the most popular sport in the country. The [Algerian national football team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algeria_national_football_team "Algeria national football team"), known as the Desert Foxes, has a strong fan base and has achieved success both domestically and internationally.[\[298\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Amara_Bouandel_2022_pp._735%E2%80%93746-305)[\[299\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Liverpool_University_Press_2017_p.-306)

Algeria has a long history in other sports such as [athletics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Athletics_in_Algeria "Athletics in Algeria"), [boxing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boxing_in_Algeria "Boxing in Algeria"), [volleyball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volleyball_in_Algeria "Volleyball in Algeria"), [handball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Handball_in_Algeria "Handball in Algeria") and the study of [martial arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martial_arts_in_Algeria "Martial arts in Algeria").[\[300\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Algeria_b859-307) Algerian athletes have [competed in the Olympic Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algeria_at_the_Olympics "Algeria at the Olympics") and have won medals in various events.[\[301\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Verlete_Sports_2020_a344-308) Many sports clubs and organisations exist in Algeria to promote and develop sports among young people.[\[302\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-SmartScraper_2024_d760-309) The [Ministry of Youth and Sports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Youth_and_Sports "Ministry of Youth and Sports") in Algeria manages sport-related activities.[\[303\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Devex_e776-310)

## Explanatory notes

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Algeria\&action=edit\&section=38 "Edit section: Explanatory notes")]

1. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_ref-4)** The [Algerian constitutional amendment of 2016](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algerian_constitutional_amendment_of_2016 "Algerian constitutional amendment of 2016") officialized Berber as Algeria's second "official" language. The revised constitution also created the [Algerian Academy of Amazigh Language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algerian_Academy_of_Amazigh_Language "Algerian Academy of Amazigh Language"), which is responsible for promoting Berber "in view of cementing, in the future, its official language status".[\[3\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-3)
2. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_ref-6)** The official languages are [Modern Standard Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modern_Standard_Arabic "Modern Standard Arabic") and, since 2016, [Standard Algerian Berber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Standard_Algerian_Berber "Standard Algerian Berber").[\[4\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-5) Algerian Arabic is the [spoken language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spoken_language "Spoken language") used by the vast majority of the population. Other [Arabic dialects and minority languages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Languages_of_Algeria "Languages of Algeria") are spoken regionally.
3. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_ref-7)** see [French language in Algeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_language_in_Algeria "French language in Algeria")
4. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_ref-8)** see [English language in Algeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_language_in_Algeria "English language in Algeria")
5. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_ref-17)** [/ælˈdʒɪəriə/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/1/16/En-Algeria-pronunciation.ogg/En-Algeria-pronunciation.ogg.mp3 "Play audio")[ⓘ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:En-Algeria-pronunciation.ogg "File:En-Algeria-pronunciation.ogg") [*al-JEER-ee-ə*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); [Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_language "Arabic language"): الجزائر, [romanized](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanization_of_Arabic "Romanization of Arabic"): *al-Jazāʾir*, Arabic: [\[al.d͡ʒazaːʔir\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Arabic "Help:IPA/Arabic") [](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/9/9a/LL-Q13955_%28ara%29-Reda_Kerbouche-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%AC%D8%B2%D8%A7%D8%A6%D8%B1.wav/LL-Q13955_%28ara%29-Reda_Kerbouche-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%AC%D8%B2%D8%A7%D8%A6%D8%B1.wav.mp3 "Play audio")[ⓘ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:LL-Q13955_\(ara\)-Reda_Kerbouche-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%AC%D8%B2%D8%A7%D8%A6%D8%B1.wav "File:LL-Q13955 (ara)-Reda Kerbouche-الجزائر.wav"); French: *Algérie*
6. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_ref-18)** [Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_language "Arabic language"): الجمهورية الجزائرية الديمقراطية الشعبية, [romanized](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanization_of_Arabic "Romanization of Arabic"): *al-Jumhūriyyah al-Jazāʾiriyyah ad-Dīmuqrāṭiyyah ash‑Shaʿbiyyah*; French: *République algérienne démocratique et populaire*. Formerly also rendered as the **Democratic and Popular Republic of Algeria** in English, as seen on the [1981 Algiers Accords](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algiers_Accords "Algiers Accords").
7. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_ref-transcription_27-0)** The transcription of Tamazight in the Tifinagh alphabet is not [codified](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Codification_\(linguistics\) "Codification (linguistics)").[\[20\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-El_Watan_2020-26)
